]
\node[stage,fill=blue!4] (down) at (0,0) {
  \textbf{Downward pass: $(a,\frac43 b)$}\\[3pt]
  Plus peaks stay fixed.\\
  $I_-$ has no plus claims.\\[3pt]
  At stopping, each minus witness\\
  claiming outside $I_-$ has\\
  $b$-mass $>3/4$ at gaps $<q$.
};
\node[stage,fill=orange!6,right=9mm of down] (up) {
  \textbf{Upward pass: $(\frac43 a,b)$}\\[3pt]
  Minus peaks stay fixed.\\
  $I_+$ has no minus claims.\\[3pt]
  At stopping, each plus witness\\
  claiming outside $I_+$ has\\
  $a$-mass $>3/4$ at gaps $<q$.
};
\draw[flow] (down.east) -- (up.west);
\node[align=center,font=\small,below=6mm of up] (persist)
  {The earlier minus-mass bound persists:\\
   every minus gap is nonincreasing.};
\draw[flow] (up.south) -- (persist.north);
\node[stage,text width=10.5cm,align=center,fill=black!3,
  below=27mm of $(down.south)!0.5!(up.south)$] (wide) {
  \textbf{Provisional call: width $D/2$, masses $(a,b)$}\\[3pt]
  Every gap $<q$ is a child claim, since $q<D/64$.\\
  Each remaining witness has mass $>3/4$\\
  receiving its sign.
};
\draw[flow] (down.south) -- ++(0,-1.28) -| (wide.north west);
\draw[flow] (persist.south) -- ++(0,-.15) -| (wide.north east);
\node[align=center,below=6mm of wide] (last) {
  Multiply masses at matching provisional signs by $4/5$,\\
  and at opposite signs by $8/5$.  The witness's total mass becomes $<1$.
};
\draw[flow] (wide.south) -- (last.north);
\end{tikzpicture}

Visual witness description of PDF page 15 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Flowchart diagram showing the interaction between passes:
- Top-left box: "Downward pass: $(a, \frac{4}{3}b)$" with text: "Plus peaks stay fixed.", "$I_-$ has no plus claims.", "At stopping, each minus witness claiming outside $I_-$ has $b$-mass $> 3/4$ at gaps $< q$."
- Top-right box: "Upward pass: $(\frac{4}{3}a, b)$" with text: "Minus peaks stay fixed.", "$I_+$ has no minus claims.", "At stopping, each plus witness claiming outside $I_+$ has $a$-mass $> 3/4$ at gaps $< q$."
- A horizontal arrow points from the "Downward pass" box to the "Upward pass" box.
- Below the "Upward pass" box is text: "The earlier minus-mass bound persists: every minus gap is nonincreasing."
- Arrows lead from the bottom of the "Downward pass" box and from below the upward pass text into a central box:
  - Middle box: "Provisional call: width $D/2$, masses $(a, b)$" with text: "Every gap $< q$ is a child claim, since $q < D/64$.", "Each remaining witness has mass $> 3/4$ receiving its sign."
- A vertical downward arrow leads from the middle box to bottom text: "Multiply masses at matching provisional signs by $4/5$, and at opposite signs by $8/5$. The witness's total mass becomes $< 1$."
```


<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Mass reweighting and completion of the proof

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-label-up-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-label-up</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:labeling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:labeling">6</a>.* Termination follows from Lemma <a href="#lem:label-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:label-rank">9</a>; we prove the labeling guarantee by induction on the same rank. In the mass base case, an index labeled $+$ has $b_i>1$, so no minus witness can contain that index in its support or claim it. At an index labeled $-$ one has $b_i\le1$ and $a_i b_i>1$, hence $a_i>1$; no plus witness can claim that index. This proves both guarantees.

In the width base case, Lemma <a href="#lem:label-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:label-comparison">7</a> gives $y\le t^*\le z$ for all witnesses. A plus claim forces $t_i^*\ge u_i-P>A_i+D/2$; a minus claim forces $t_i^*\le A_i+P<A_i+D/2$. Thus the returned signs are correct.

<a id="eq:label-plus-survives"></a>
For a nonbase call, assume correctness of all its children. Let $Q_+=\{i:\ell_i^0=+\}$ and $Q_-=\{i:\ell_i^0=-\}$ be the provisional sign sets. Consider a plus witness $y$ that has a claim outside $I_+$. By Equation <a href="#eq:label-final-plus-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-final-plus-mass">[eq:label-final-plus-mass]</a>, the set $G_y=\{i:g_i^y(k^f)<q\}$ has $a$-mass greater than $3/4$. It lies in $S_y$ by Lemma <a href="#lem:label-translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:label-translation">10</a>. Apply that lemma with width $r=D/2$ to the provisional call. Its plus masses are unchanged, so the translated vector is a plus witness. Since $$q=D/128<D/64=r/32,$$ every index in $G_y$ is a claim of this translated witness. All these indices belong to $Q_+$ by the child’s guarantee. Consequently $$\bar a(S_y)
   =\frac85 a(S_y)-\frac45 a(S_y\cap Q_+)
   <\frac85-\frac45\cdot\frac34=1.
 \label{eq:label-plus-survives}$$ The parent vector $y$ is still a subsolution in the unchanged box, so it is a plus witness for the continuation.

<a id="eq:label-minus-survives"></a>
For a minus witness $z$ having a claim outside $I_-$, the set $G_z=\{i:g_i^z(k^f)<q\}$ has $b$-mass greater than $3/4$ by Equation <a href="#eq:label-final-minus-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-final-minus-mass">[eq:label-final-minus-mass]</a>. It lies in $S_z$. The width-$D/2$ minus translation is a witness for the provisional call because the minus masses are unchanged; every index in $G_z$ is one of its claims, and hence belongs to $Q_-$. Therefore $$\bar b(S_z)
   =\frac85 b(S_z)-\frac45 b(S_z\cap Q_-)
   <\frac85-\frac45\cdot\frac34=1.
 \label{eq:label-minus-survives}$$ The same vector $z$ is a minus witness for the continuation.

It remains to check every claim after the overrides. Let $y$ claim $i$. Lemma <a href="#lem:label-down" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:label-down">11</a> gives $i\notin I_-$. If $i\in I_+$, it receives the plus override. If $i\notin I_+$, Equation <a href="#eq:label-plus-survives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-plus-survives">[eq:label-plus-survives]</a> applies to $y$, the continuation labels $i$ by $+$, and no override changes it. Now let $z$ claim $i$. Lemma <a href="#lem:label-up" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:label-up">12</a> gives $i\notin I_+$. If $i\in I_-$, it receives the minus override. Otherwise Equation <a href="#eq:label-minus-survives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-minus-survives">[eq:label-minus-survives]</a> applies to $z$, and the continuation’s minus label is unchanged. In particular $I_-\cap I_+$ contains no claim of either sign, so the stated priority between overrides causes no conflict. This completes the induction. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:application"></a>
### From labels to an exact decision

We now apply Theorem <a href="#thm:labeling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:labeling">6</a> to a scaled discounted map. The labels need not locate its fixed point directly. Instead, we construct two integer comparison vectors on opposite sides of that point. Their coordinatewise distance is small, so a label excluding one vector from a boundary strip also allows that boundary to move past a smaller strip without excluding either vector. Repeating this operation gives the accuracy required by Theorem <a href="#thm:discount" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:discount">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### Two nearby integer comparison vectors

<a id="eq:application-parameters"></a>
<a id="eq:application-shift"></a>
<a id="eq:comparison-vectors"></a>
Use the integers $W,Q,A_0,H$ of Section <a href="#sec:discount" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:discount">2</a>, and set $$\begin{gathered}
 N=32H^2,\qquad \lambda=1/N=\lambda_0,\qquad \gamma=1-1/N,\\
 K=256(N+1),\qquad S=16HK,\qquad J=SW+N+1.
\end{gathered}
\label{eq:application-parameters}$$ Let $$F(t)=S f_\lambda(t/S),\qquad x=S v(\lambda).$$ Then $F(x)=x$, the map $F$ is monotone, and $$F(t+c\mathbf 1)=F(t)+\gamma c\mathbf 1\qquad(t\in\mathbb R^n,\ c\in\mathbb R).
\label{eq:application-shift}$$ Thus $F$ has the properties used in Section <a href="#sec:labeling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:labeling">3</a>. Define the integer vectors $$Y=\lfloor x\rfloor-N\mathbf 1,
 \qquad Z=\lceil x\rceil+N\mathbf 1.
\label{eq:comparison-vectors}$$ Floors and ceilings are coordinatewise; in particular, a floor of a negative number is taken toward minus infinity. These vectors are used only in the proof. The algorithm will not compute them.

<a id="stmt-lem-comparison-vectors"></a>
<div id="lem:comparison-vectors" class="lemma">

**Lemma 13**. *The vectors in <a href="#eq:comparison-vectors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison-vectors">[eq:comparison-vectors]</a> satisfy $$F(Y)\ge Y,\qquad F(Z)\le Z,\qquad Y\le x\le Z,
 \qquad \|Y\|_\infty,\|Z\|_\infty\le J.$$ For every coordinate $i$, <a id="eq:comparison-gap"></a>$$Z_i-Y_i\le 2(N+1)=K/128.
\label{eq:comparison-gap}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-comparison-vectors"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-comparison-vectors</summary>

*Proof.* The inequalities $x-(N+1)\mathbf 1\le Y\le x-N\mathbf 1$ and $x+N\mathbf 1\le Z\le x+(N+1)\mathbf 1$, together with $\gamma(N+1)=N-1/N\le N$, give $$F(Y)\ge x-\gamma(N+1)\mathbf 1\ge x-N\mathbf 1\ge Y$$ and $$F(Z)\le x+\gamma(N+1)\mathbf 1\le x+N\mathbf 1\le Z.$$ Here we used monotonicity and <a href="#eq:application-shift" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:application-shift">[eq:application-shift]</a>. The remaining conclusions follow from these defining bounds and $\|x\|_\infty\le SW$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
#### Shrinking the box

<a id="eq:initial-box"></a>
Recall that the permitted widths are $D=h2^d$ with $128\le h\le255$ and integers $d\ge0$. Start with $$A=-J\mathbf 1,\qquad D=D_0=128\cdot2^{d_0},
 \label{eq:initial-box}$$ where $d_0$ is the least nonnegative integer for which $D_0\ge2J$. The box $[A,A+D\mathbf 1]$ contains $Y$ and $Z$.

<a id="eq:label-exclusions"></a>
While $D>K$, call $\mathcal L(A,D,a,b)$ with $a_i=b_i=1/n$ for all $i$. Put $u=A+D\mathbf 1$ and $P=D/32$. Within the current box, $Y$ is a subsolution and $Z$ is a supersolution, and their support masses are at most one. Consequently, the labeling guarantee has the following contrapositive form: $$\begin{aligned}
 \text{label }+\text{ at }i&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Z_i>A_i+P,\\
 \text{label }-\text{ at }i&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Y_i<u_i-P.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:label-exclusions}$$ For example, $Z_i\le A_i+P$ would be a minus claim and would require a minus label.

<a id="eq:shrink-slack"></a>
Let $D'$ be the next smaller permitted width. More explicitly, <a id="eq:next-width"></a>$$D'=\begin{cases}
 (h-1)2^d,&h>128,\\
 255\cdot2^{d-1},&h=128.
 \end{cases}
 \label{eq:next-width}$$ The second case has $d\ge1$, because the loop condition implies $D>K>255$. Set $\delta=D-D'$. In the first case $\delta/D=1/h\le1/128$; in the second case this ratio is $1/256$. Thus $$0<\delta\le D/128,
 \qquad Z_i-Y_i\le K/128<D/128.
 \label{eq:shrink-slack}$$ Define $$A'_i=\begin{cases}
 A_i+\delta,&\text{if the label is }+,\\
 A_i,&\text{if the label is }-.
 \end{cases}$$ Replace $(A,D)$ by $(A',D')$.

<a id="stmt-lem-box-invariant"></a>
<div id="lem:box-invariant" class="lemma">

**Lemma 14**. *Every box in this iteration contains $Y$ and $Z$. The iteration makes at most $128(d_0+1)$ calls to $\mathcal L$ and ends with $D\le K$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-box-invariant"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-box-invariant</summary>

*Proof.* The initial containment was established above. At a plus coordinate, <a href="#eq:label-exclusions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:label-exclusions">[eq:label-exclusions]</a> and <a href="#eq:shrink-slack" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shrink-slack">[eq:shrink-slack]</a> give $$Y_i>Z_i-D/128>A_i+D/32-D/128
       =A_i+3D/128\ge A_i+\delta.$$ The lower endpoint can therefore increase by $\delta$; the upper endpoint remains unchanged. At a minus coordinate the same inequalities give $$Z_i<Y_i+D/128<u_i-D/32+D/128
       =u_i-3D/128\le u_i-\delta.$$ The upper endpoint can decrease by $\delta$; the lower endpoint remains unchanged. This proves the induction step for both comparison vectors.

There are only $128$ permitted mantissas at any exponent, and the exponent never increases. The strictly decreasing sequence of widths therefore has at most $128(d_0+1)$ terms before reaching the stopping range, which contains all permitted widths at exponent zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:final-error"></a>
<a id="eq:output-test"></a>
At termination, $Y\le x\le Z$ and the invariant imply $A_i\le x_i\le A_i+D$. Hence <a id="eq:fixedpoint-error"></a>$$\left|\frac{A_i}{S}-v_i(\lambda)\right|
 \le\frac KS=\frac1{16H}.
 \label{eq:fixedpoint-error}$$ Theorem <a href="#thm:discount" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:discount">2</a> now gives $$\left|\frac{A_i}{S}-\operatorname{val}(i)\right|\le\frac1{8H}.
 \label{eq:final-error}$$ Output exactly the indices satisfying $$\frac{A_i}{S}\ge-\frac1{2H},
 \quad\text{equivalently}\quad A_i\ge-8K.
 \label{eq:output-test}$$ If $\operatorname{val}(i)=0$, <a href="#eq:final-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:final-error">[eq:final-error]</a> puts $A_i/S$ at least $-1/(8H)$, so the index is included. If $\operatorname{val}(i)>0$, the same conclusion holds. If $\operatorname{val}(i)<0$, the value gap in Theorem <a href="#thm:discount" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:discount">2</a> gives $A_i/S\le-1/H+1/(8H)=-7/(8H)$, so the index is excluded. This proves exact correctness, including equality at zero.

<a id="subsec:bit-complexity"></a>
#### Bit complexity and uniformity

It remains to show that the potentially large integer widths do not produce a large recursion tree, and that every local computation has polynomial bit cost. The first point uses the mass-product rank of Section <a href="#sec:labeling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:labeling">3</a>; the second uses the explicit binary encoding.

Since every vertex has an outgoing edge and the input is explicit, $n,|E|=O(L+1)$. The binary lengths of the probabilities and rewards give $$\log_2 Q=O(L+1),\qquad \log_2 W=O(L+1),\qquad
 \log_2 A_0=O((L+2)^2).$$ In particular, all integers in <a href="#eq:application-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:application-parameters">[eq:application-parameters]</a> and <a href="#eq:initial-box" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-box">[eq:initial-box]</a> have $O((L+2)^2)$ bits, and $d_0=O((L+2)^2)$. Reading the input and forming these integers by ordinary exact arithmetic take polynomial time. Every recursive box lies in its parent, and every outer box lies in the initial box. Thus all box endpoints, pivot coordinates, and integer arguments to $F$ have polynomial binary length.

For an integer vector $t$, the quantities used to evaluate $F_i(t)$ are <a id="eq:exact-evaluation"></a>$$\frac{S w(e)+(N-1)t_j}{N},\qquad e:i\longrightarrow j.
 \label{eq:exact-evaluation}$$ Max and Min take exact extrema. At a chance vertex write the encoded probability as $p(e)=c_e/d_e$ with $d_e>0$. Its weighted sum can be formed with the common denominator $QN$ and integer numerator $$\sum_{e:i\to j}c_e\frac{Q}{d_e}
           \bigl(Sw(e)+(N-1)t_j\bigr).$$ Every $d_e$ divides $Q$, even when input fractions are not reduced. This formula gives polynomial bit lengths and polynomial bit cost for an evaluation and its coordinatewise floors. Zero probabilities and parallel edges require no special handling.

Consider one outer call, whose initial masses are $a_i=b_i=1/n$. For a recursive call put $$\mu=\min_i a_i b_i,\qquad \rho=\frac{32}{25},\qquad
 B(\mu)=\min\{j\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}:\rho^j\mu>1\}.$$ The initial value $B_0=B(1/n^2)$ is $O(\log(n+1))$; this includes $n=1$, for which $B_0=1$. Every recursive edge decreases $d+B$. Only the provisional-label child can preserve $B$, and it decreases $d$ by one. Each other child decreases $B$ by at least one and does not increase $d$. Thus the depth is at most $$\Lambda=d_0+B_0,$$ and any root-to-node path contains at most $B_0$ edges decreasing $B$. At each node there is at most one $B$-preserving child and at most $$s_*=2(1024n+1)+1=2048n+3$$ other children: the two pivot passes account for the first term and the reweighted call for the final one.

<a id="eq:tree-bound"></a>
A path of length $\ell$ with $j$ edges decreasing $B$ is specified by their $j$ positions and at most $s_*$ choices at each such position; the other child, if present, is unique. The total number of nodes is therefore at most $$\begin{aligned}
 \sum_{\ell=0}^{\Lambda}\sum_{j=0}^{\min(\ell,B_0)}
       \binom{\ell}{j}s_*^j
 &\le (\Lambda+1)(B_0+1)(1+\Lambda s_*)^{B_0} \\
 &=2^{O((\log_2(L+2))^2)}.
 \label{eq:tree-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ The distinction between these two kinds of children is essential: the depth may be polynomial in $L$, but branching choices occur only $O(\log(n+1))$ times on any path.

All masses can be stored as unreduced positive fractions. An edge that changes masses multiplies each by one of a fixed set of rational constants, and so increases its numerator and denominator lengths by at most a constant. The other edge leaves masses unchanged. Thus the mass lengths are polynomial (indeed $O(\log(n+1))$), and every comparison, update, and support-independent base test has polynomial bit cost. The algorithm does not compute $B$; it is used only for this analysis.

Outside its children, a nonbase call performs $O(n+1)$ pivot updates and associated vector operations, all of polynomial bit cost. In a width base case, $d<12$ gives $D\le255\cdot2^{11}$, an absolute constant, so the clipped-floor iteration uses at most $1+nD$ polynomial-cost evaluations. The mass base case is also polynomial. Consequently <a href="#eq:tree-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-bound">[eq:tree-bound]</a> bounds the bit cost of one outer call up to a polynomial factor. Lemma <a href="#lem:box-invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:box-invariant">14</a> bounds the number of outer calls by a polynomial as well.

Finally, all loops, tie rules, integer operations, and recursive calls are specified by finite algorithms independent of the input. A depth-first implementation stores a stack of polynomial depth whose individual records have polynomial length. Scanning such a stack or copying a record on a deterministic Turing machine costs at most a polynomial factor per local operation. The final integer tests <a href="#eq:output-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:output-test">[eq:output-test]</a> and the output take polynomial time. Absorbing these factors into <a href="#eq:tree-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-bound">[eq:tree-bound]</a> proves the bound $2^{C(\log_2(L+2))^2}$ for one absolute constant $C$. This completes the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

      "caption": "The two passes preserve opposite peaks and force opposite mass inequalities. A remaining witness means one with a claim outside its own override set: $I_{+}$ for plus witnesses and $I_{−}$ for minus witnesses. The upward pass preserves the mass inequality obtained during the downward pass.",
      "figure_id": "fig:labeling-passes",

      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:labeling-passes",
          "exact_quote": "The two passes preserve opposite peaks and force opposite\n mass inequalities.  A remaining witness means one with a claim\n outside its own override set: $I_+$ for plus witnesses and $I_-$\n for minus witnesses.  The upward pass preserves the",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  font=\\small,\n  stage/.style={draw=black!60,rounded corners=2pt,align=left,\n    text width=5.7cm,inner sep=8pt},\n  flow/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},thick,draw=black!65}\n]\n\\node[stage,fill=blue!4] (down) at (0,0) {\n  \\textbf{Downward pass: $(a,\\frac43 b)$}\\\\[3pt]\n  Plus peaks stay fixed.\\\\\n  $I_-$ has no plus claims.\\\\[3pt]\n  At stopping, each minus witness\\\\\n  claiming outside $I_-$ has\\\\\n  $b$-mass $>3/4$ at gaps $<q$.\n};\n\\node[stage,fill=orange!6,right=9mm of down] (up) {\n  \\textbf{Upward pass: $(\\frac43 a,b)$}\\\\[3pt]\n  Minus peaks stay fixed.\\\\\n  $I_+$ has no minus claims.\\\\[3pt]\n  At stopping, each plus witness\\\\\n  claiming outside $I_+$ has\\\\\n  $a$-mass $>3/4$ at gaps $<q$.\n};\n\\draw[flow] (down.east) -- (up.west);\n\\node[align=center,font=\\small,below=6mm of up] (persist)\n  {The earlier minus-mass bound persists:\\\\\n   every minus gap is nonincreasing.};\n\\draw[flow] (up.south) -- (persist.north);\n\\node[stage,text width=10.5cm,align=center,fill=black!3,\n  below=27mm of $(down.south)!0.5!(up.south)$] (wide) {\n  \\textbf{Provisional call: width $D/2$, masses $(a,b)$}\\\\[3pt]\n  Every gap $<q$ is a child claim, since $q<D/64$.\\\\\n  Each remaining witness has mass $>3/4$\\\\\n  receiving its sign.\n};\n\\draw[flow] (down.south) -- ++(0,-1.28) -| (wide.north west);\n\\draw[flow] (persist.south) -- ++(0,-.15) -| (wide.north east);\n\\node[align=center,below=6mm of wide] (last) {\n  Multiply masses at matching provisional signs by $4/5$,\\\\\n  and at opposite signs by $8/5$.  The witness's total mass becomes $<1$.\n};\n\\draw[flow] (wide.south) -- (last.north);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram showing the interaction between passes:\n- Top-left box: \"Downward pass: $(a, \\frac{4}{3}b)$\" with text: \"Plus peaks stay fixed.\", \"$I_-$ has no plus claims.\", \"At stopping, each minus witness claiming outside $I_-$ has $b$-mass $> 3/4$ at gaps $< q$.\"\n- Top-right box: \"Upward pass: $(\\frac{4}{3}a, b)$\" with text: \"Minus peaks stay fixed.\", \"$I_+$ has no minus claims.\", \"At stopping, each plus witness claiming outside $I_+$ has $a$-mass $> 3/4$ at gaps $< q$.\"\n- A horizontal arrow points from the \"Downward pass\" box to the \"Upward pass\" box.\n- Below the \"Upward pass\" box is text: \"The earlier minus-mass bound persists: every minus gap is nonincreasing.\"\n- Arrows lead from the bottom of the \"Downward pass\" box and from below the upward pass text into a central box:\n  - Middle box: \"Provisional call: width $D/2$, masses $(a, b)$\" with text: \"Every gap $< q$ is a child claim, since $q < D/64$.\", \"Each remaining witness has mass $> 3/4$ receiving its sign.\"\n- A vertical downward arrow leads from the middle box to bottom text: \"Multiply masses at matching provisional signs by $4/5$, and at opposite signs by $8/5$. The witness's total mass becomes $< 1$.\""
    }
